        await input("camProgramName", "Native plate")
        await input("projectName", "Machined plate")
        await click("[data-testid='workspace-save-project']")
        const saved = await app.wait(() => useCamStore.getState().savedProgram, 30_000)
        const row = await app.state.runtime.run(app.client.toolpaths.get({ params: { id: saved.id } }))
        expect(row.name).toBe("Native plate")
        expect(row.gcode).toBe(artifact.nc)
        expect(row.drawingId).toBe(useDocumentStore.getState().drawingId)
        expect(row.data).toMatchObject({ format: "cadcamfun-cam", version: 1, kernelArtifact: { identity: artifact.identity }, program: artifact.program })
        const project = await app.sidecar.call(`/api/projects/${saved.projectId}`)
        expect(project.body.isPublic).toBe(false)
        const drawing = await app.sidecar.call(`/api/drawings/${saved.drawingId}`)
        expect(drawing.body.data.elements).toEqual([plate])
        await app.state.runtime.run(app.client.toolpaths.update({ params: { id: saved.id }, payload: { data: { ...(row.data as Record<string, unknown>), customNote: "Keep this metadata", sourceRevision: "original-revision", verification: { original: true } } } }))
        const changed = `${artifact.nc}\n; edited in CAM`
        useCamStore.getState().setGcode(changed)
        await click("button[title='Save G-Code']")
        await click("[data-testid='workspace-save-as-project']")
        expect(document.querySelector<HTMLSelectElement>("select[name='camSaveDestination']")!.value).toBe("update")
        await click("[data-testid='workspace-save-project']")
        await app.wait(() => !document.querySelector("[data-testid='workspace-project-dialog']"))
        const updated = await app.state.runtime.run(app.client.toolpaths.get({ params: { id: saved.id } }))
        expect(updated.gcode).toBe(changed)
        expect(updated.data).toMatchObject({ customNote: "Keep this metadata", gcode: changed })
        expect((updated.data as Record<string, unknown>)["kernelArtifact"]).toBeUndefined()
        expect((updated.data as Record<string, unknown>)["sourceRevision"]).toBeUndefined()
        expect((updated.data as Record<string, unknown>)["verification"]).toBeUndefined()
        const versions = await app.state.runtime.run(app.client.toolpathExtras.listVersions({ params: { id: saved.id } }))
        expect(versions).toHaveLength(1)
        expect(versions[0]!.gcode).toBe(artifact.nc)
        expect(versions[0]!.data).toMatchObject({ kernelArtifact: { identity: artifact.identity }, customNote: "Keep this metadata" })
        await app.state.runtime.run(app.client.toolpathExtras.restoreVersion({ params: { id: saved.id }, payload: { versionId: versions[0]!.id } }))
        view.dispose(); resetStores()
        view = app.visit(`/cam?loadToolpath=${saved.id}`)
        await app.wait(() => useCamStore.getState().savedProgram?.id === saved.id)
        expect(useCamStore.getState().gcode).toBe(artifact.nc)
        expect(useCamStore.getState().program).toEqual(artifact.program)
        expect(useCamStore.getState().kernelArtifact?.identity).toEqual(artifact.identity)
        await click("button[title='Save G-Code']")
        await click("[data-testid='workspace-save-as-project']")
        expect(document.querySelector<HTMLAnchorElement>("a[href='/toolpaths/" + saved.id + "']")?.textContent).toBe("Manage saved toolpath")
      } finally { view.dispose() }
    })
    
    test("generator Save uses the same project flow, without overwriting an existing CAD drawing", async () => {
      const project = await app.state.runtime.run(app.client.projects.create({ payload: { name: "Existing machining" } }))
      const drawing = await app.state.runtime.run(app.client.drawings.createForProject({ params: { id: project.id }, payload: { name: "Stored drawing", data: { elements: [{ ...plate, width: 40 }] } } }))
      useDocumentStore.getState().setDocument({ projectId: project.id, drawingId: drawing.id })

      const view = app.visit("/cam")
      try {
        const button = await app.wait(() => [...document.querySelectorAll<HTMLButtonElement>("button")].find((button) => button.textContent?.includes("Save as Toolpath")), 30_000)
        button.click()
        await app.wait(() => document.querySelector<HTMLSelectElement>("select[name='camSaveProject']")?.value === project.id)
        await app.wait(() => !document.querySelector<HTMLButtonElement>("[data-testid='workspace-save-project']")?.disabled)
        await click("[data-testid='workspace-save-project']")

        expect(saved.projectId).toBe(project.id)
        expect(saved.drawingId).toBe(drawing.id)
        const stored = await app.sidecar.call(`/api/drawings/${drawing.id}`)
        expect(stored.body.data.elements[0].width).toBe(40)

        expect(rows).toHaveLength(1)
        expect(rows[0]!.gcode).toBe(artifact.nc)
        expect(rows[0]!.data).toMatchObject({ program: artifact.program })
        expect(localStorage.getItem("gcodeFromToolpathGenerator")).toBeNull()
      } finally { view.dispose() }
    })
    
    test("CAM Undo, Redo and History restore NC, native paths and settings together and discard abandoned redo", async () => {
      const { useCamHistoryStore, flushCamHistory, resetCamHistory } = await import("../src/features/cam/store/camHistoryStore.ts")
      prepareNative(); resetCamHistory()
      const view = app.visit("/cam")
      try {
        const editor = await app.wait(() => document.querySelector<HTMLTextAreaElement>("textarea"), 30_000)
        expect(document.querySelector<HTMLButtonElement>("button[title='Undo']")!.disabled).toBe(true)
        const changed = `${artifact.nc}\n; manual edit`
        editor.value = changed; editor.dispatchEvent(new window.Event("input", { bubbles: true }))
        await app.wait(() => useCamHistoryStore.getState().index > 0)
        await click("button[title='Undo']")
        expect(useCamStore.getState().gcode).toBe(artifact.nc)
        expect(useCamStore.getState().program).toEqual(artifact.program)
        expect(useCamStore.getState().kernelRequest?.id).toBe("save-fixture")
        await click("button[title='Redo']")
        expect(useCamStore.getState().gcode).toBe(changed)
        const feed = useGeneratorStore.getState().settings.feedrate
        useGeneratorStore.getState().patchSettings({ feedrate: feed + 100 }); flushCamHistory()
        expect(useGeneratorStore.getState().settings.feedrate).toBe(feed + 100)
        window.dispatchEvent(new window.KeyboardEvent("keydown", { key: "z", ctrlKey: true, bubbles: true, cancelable: true }))
        expect(useGeneratorStore.getState().settings.feedrate).toBe(feed)
        useCamStore.getState().setGcode(`${artifact.nc}\n; different branch`); flushCamHistory()
        expect(document.querySelector<HTMLButtonElement>("button[title='Redo']")!.disabled).toBe(true)
        const count = useCamHistoryStore.getState().entries.length
        useCamStore.getState().setPlaybackTime(2); flushCamHistory()
        expect(useCamHistoryStore.getState().entries).toHaveLength(count)
        await click("button[title='CAM History']")
        expect(document.querySelector("[data-testid='cam-history-dialog']")?.textContent).toContain("Initial program")
        const first = document.querySelector<HTMLButtonElement>("[data-testid='cam-history-dialog'] ol button")!
        first.click()
        expect(useCamStore.getState().gcode).toBe(artifact.nc)
        expect(useCamStore.getState().program).toEqual(artifact.program)
        expect(useCamStore.getState().playing).toBe(false)
      } finally { view.dispose() }
    })

    import { createHash } from "node:crypto"
    import { BadRequest, CamKernelResult, CamProgramDocument, type CamKernelRequest } from "@cadcamfun/api"
    import { cam } from "@cadcamfun/core"
    import { Effect, Schema } from "effect"
    import { elementsOf } from "./ai-elements.ts"
    
    const record = (value: unknown): Record<string, unknown> => value !== null && typeof value === "object" && !Array.isArray(value) ? value as Record<string, unknown> : {}
    
    /** Preserve the existing stored-operation revision algorithm while sharing it with HTTP saves. */
    export const drawingRevision = (loaded: { readonly id: string; readonly elements: ReadonlyArray<Record<string, unknown>>; readonly data: Record<string, unknown> }): string => {
      const canonical = (value: unknown): unknown => Array.isArray(value) ? value.map(canonical) : value !== null && typeof value === "object"
        ? Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(value).sort(([a], [b]) => a.localeCompare(b)).map(([key, child]) => [key, canonical(child)])) : value
      return createHash("sha256").update(JSON.stringify(canonical({ drawingId: loaded.id, elements: loaded.elements, stock: loaded.data["stock"] ?? null }))).digest("hex")
    }
    
    /** The same request adapter serves preview generation and persistence revalidation. */
    export const runKernelRequest = Effect.fn("cam.runKernelRequest")(function*(payload: CamKernelRequest) {
      const machine = payload.machine !== undefined ? cam.kernel.findMachine(payload.machine) : undefined
      if (payload.machine !== undefined && machine === undefined) return yield* new BadRequest({ message: `Unknown machine preset '${payload.machine}'` })
      const result = yield* Effect.try({ try: () => {
        const meshes = payload.stock !== undefined ? cam.model.partMeshes(payload.elements, { tolerance: payload.tolerance ?? 0.01 }) : undefined
        const bottom = meshes !== undefined ? Math.min(...meshes.meshes.map((mesh) => cam.model.meshBounds(mesh).min[2])) : 0
        const stock = payload.stock
        return cam.kernel.runJob({
          schemaVersion: payload.schemaVersion, id: payload.id, name: payload.name, material: payload.material,
          part: { kind: "elements", elements: payload.elements }, ...(machine !== undefined ? { machine } : {}),
          ...(stock !== undefined ? { stock: { min: { x: stock.x - stock.width / 2, y: stock.y - stock.height / 2, z: bottom }, max: { x: stock.x + stock.width / 2, y: stock.y + stock.height / 2, z: bottom + stock.depth } } } : {}),
          ...(payload.origin !== undefined ? { origin: payload.origin } : {}), ...(payload.workOffset !== undefined ? { workOffset: payload.workOffset } : {}),
          quality: { ...(payload.finishAllowance !== undefined ? { finishAllowance: payload.finishAllowance } : {}), ...(payload.scallop !== undefined ? { scallop: payload.scallop } : {}), ...(payload.tolerance !== undefined ? { tolerance: payload.tolerance } : {}), ...(payload.resolution !== undefined ? { resolution: payload.resolution } : {}) }
        })
      }, catch: () => new BadRequest({ message: "The CAM kernel request could not be evaluated" }) })
      return yield* Schema.decodeUnknownEffect(CamKernelResult)(result).pipe(Effect.mapError(() => new BadRequest({ message: "The CAM kernel returned an invalid artifact" })))
    })
    
    const same = (a: unknown, b: unknown): boolean => cam.kernel.canonicalJson(a) === cam.kernel.canonicalJson(b)
    export const isPortableProgram = (data: unknown): boolean => record(data)["format"] === "cadcamfun-cam"
    
    /** Validate recognized new payloads without changing the legacy free-form data contract. */
    export const prepareCamProgramData = Effect.fn("cam.prepareProgramData")(function*(input: {
      readonly data: unknown; readonly gcode: string | null | undefined; readonly previous?: unknown;
      readonly drawing?: { readonly id: string; readonly data: unknown } | undefined
    }) {
      const data = { ...record(input.data) }, old = record(input.previous)
      if (!isPortableProgram(data)) {
        const artifact = record(data["kernelArtifact"])
        if (artifact["nc"] !== undefined && artifact["nc"] !== input.gcode) {
          for (const key of ["kernelArtifact", "kernelRequest", "sourceRevision", "verification", "response"]) delete data[key]
          return data
        }
        if (artifact["nc"] !== undefined) {
          if (!same(data["kernelArtifact"], old["kernelArtifact"] ?? null)) return yield* new BadRequest({ message: "New native artifacts require a portable CAM program and captured source request" })
          delete data["sourceRevision"]
          if (old["sourceRevision"] !== undefined) data["sourceRevision"] = old["sourceRevision"]
          return data
        }
        return input.data
      }
      let snapshot = yield* Schema.decodeUnknownEffect(CamProgramDocument)(data).pipe(Effect.mapError(() => new BadRequest({ message: "Invalid CADCAMFUN toolpath document" })))
      if (snapshot.gcode !== input.gcode) {
        if (input.previous === undefined || old["gcode"] !== snapshot.gcode) return yield* new BadRequest({ message: "Toolpath document and G-code must contain the same program" })
        // Existing editor/API updates may send only new NC and the unchanged old document.
        const code = input.gcode ?? ""
        const program = yield* Effect.try({ try: () => cam.generator.analyseGcode(code).program, catch: () => new BadRequest({ message: "The saved G-code could not be parsed" }) })
        delete data["kernelArtifact"]; delete data["kernelRequest"]
        snapshot = yield* Schema.decodeUnknownEffect(CamProgramDocument)({ ...data, gcode: code, program }).pipe(Effect.mapError(() => new BadRequest({ message: "Invalid updated toolpath document" })))
      }
      delete data["sourceRevision"]
      delete data["verification"]
      delete data["response"]
      if (snapshot.kernelArtifact) {
        let artifact = snapshot.kernelArtifact
        if (snapshot.kernelRequest) {
          const result = yield* runKernelRequest(snapshot.kernelRequest)
          if (result.status !== "ok" || result.value.release === "blocked" || !same(result.value, artifact) || !same(snapshot.program, artifact.program) || artifact.nc !== input.gcode) {
            return yield* new BadRequest({ message: "The saved kernel artifact does not match its source request and original NC" })
          }
          artifact = result.value
        } else if (!same(old["kernelArtifact"] ?? null, artifact) || artifact.nc !== input.gcode || !same(snapshot.program, artifact.program)) {
          // A legacy export can still be saved as ordinary NC; client-provided claims alone are not verification.
          delete data["kernelArtifact"]
        }
        if (data["kernelArtifact"] !== undefined) {
          data["kernelArtifact"] = artifact
          const drawing = input.drawing, source = snapshot.kernelRequest
          if (drawing && source) {
            const stored = record(drawing.data), elements = elementsOf(stored)
            const selected = source.elements.map((element) => elements.find((candidate) => candidate["id"] === element["id"]))
            if (selected.every((element) => element !== undefined) && same(selected, source.elements) && same(stored["stock"] ?? null, source.stock ?? null)) {
              data["sourceRevision"] = drawingRevision({ id: drawing.id, data: stored, elements })
            }
          } else if (old["sourceRevision"] !== undefined && same(old["kernelArtifact"], artifact)) data["sourceRevision"] = old["sourceRevision"]
        }
      } else {
        for (const key of ["kernelArtifact", "kernelRequest", "sourceRevision", "verification", "response"]) delete data[key]
      }
      const output = { ...data, ...Schema.encodeSync(CamProgramDocument)(snapshot) }
      if (data["kernelArtifact"] === undefined) {
        delete output["kernelArtifact"]; delete output["kernelRequest"]
        output["program"] = yield* Effect.try({ try: () => cam.generator.analyseGcode(snapshot.gcode).program, catch: () => new BadRequest({ message: "The saved G-code could not be parsed" }) })
      }
      return output
    })

        } catch (cause) {
          toast.error(cause instanceof Error ? cause.message : "Failed to save toolpath")
        } finally {
          setBusy(false)
        }
      }
    
      const deleteToolpath = async (): Promise<void> => {
        const current = row()
        if (!current) return
        setBusy(true)
        try {
          await run(app.client.toolpaths.remove({ params: { id: current.id } }))

        } catch (cause) {
          toast.error(cause instanceof Error ? cause.message : "Failed to delete toolpath")

      const restore = async (versionId: string): Promise<void> => {

        if (!current) return
        setRestoring(versionId)
        try {
          const updated = await run<ToolpathRow>(app.client.toolpathExtras.restoreVersion({ params: { id: current.id }, payload: { versionId } }))
          setRow(updated)
          setDraft(draftOf(updated))
          toast.success("Version restored successfully")
          await loadVersions()
        } catch (cause) {
          toast.error(cause instanceof Error ? cause.message : "Failed to restore version")
        } finally {
          setRestoring(undefined)
        }
      }
    
      const addComment = async (event: Event): Promise<void> => {
        event.preventDefault()
        const current = row()
        const text = commentText().trim()
        if (!current || text === "") return
        try {
          await run(app.client.toolpathExtras.createComment({ params: { id: current.id }, payload: { content: text } }))
          setCommentText("")

    # WP14 — Multi-operation setups (many tools, many programs)
    
    Status: **in progress**. Owner dirs: `packages/core/src/cam/program` (+ `post`, `analysis`), `packages/api/src/groups/cam-setup.ts`, `apps/sidecar/src/http/cam-setup.ts`, `apps/desktop/src/features/cam/{lib/setup.ts,store/setupStore.ts}`. Depends on WP2 (operations, posts, verifier) and WP3 (API contract).
    
    ## 1. The problem
    
    The v6 generator takes one operation per request (`POST /api/toolpaths/generate` → `GenerateRequest { settings, geometry }`), and the desktop multi-toolpath panel stitches the resulting texts together (`combineGCode`, `apps/desktop/src/features/cam/lib/multiToolpath.ts`). Concatenating full programs is not a program: each block carries its own header, `G21`, `M30` and `%`, no tool change is emitted, and no pocket number is assigned. The machine cannot run it.
    
    ## 2. The model
    
    ```
    Setup        = stock + machine + post config + grouping policy + ordered Operations
    Operation    = element ids + strategy/parameters + one Tool (+ optional pocket)
    ToolTable    = pocket (T1..Tn) -> Tool (library reference), derived from the operations
    Program      = one emitted .nc grouping operations; tool changes between them
    ```
    
    ```
    Operations ──► planPrograms ──► PlannedProgram[] ──► generateSetup ──► GeneratedProgram[] (text + IR)
                    (pure, no      (grouping, pockets,    (per operation: the existing
                     geometry)      ordering, numbering)     generator, then ONE emit per program)
    ```
    
    Two things the plan deliberately keeps apart:
    
    - **`single` grouping** — one program, tool changes inside it (what most machines want).
    - **`perTool` / `perOperation` grouping** — one file per tool / per operation (operator preference, machine memory limits).
    
    Both come from the same planner, so the UI can offer all of them without a second code path.
    
    ## 3. Contracts (`packages/core/src/cam/program`)
    
    ```ts
    // op.ts
    Operation = { id, name, elementIds: string[], params: ToolpathSettings, tool: Tool,
                  pocket?: int, enabled: boolean, fromPreviousStock: boolean }
    
    // setup.ts
    Setup = { id, name, machineType, workOffset, stock: Stock, post: PostConfig,
              grouping: "single" | "perTool" | "perOperation",
              retractBeforeToolChange: boolean, operations: Operation[] }
    PostConfig = { postId: "generic" | "fanuc" | "grbl", decimals, useCannedCycles, arcMode,
                   header, tolerance, lineNumbers?, stamp?, programNumberBase }
    
    // tooltable.ts
    ToolTableEntry = { pocket: number, tool: Tool, operationIds: string[] }
    buildToolTable(operations): { entries: ToolTableEntry[], warnings: string[] }
    assignPockets(operations): Map<operationId, pocket>      // explicit pocket, else first use
    
    // plan.ts
    PlannedProgram = { name, programNumber, toolNumbers: number[], operations: string[] }
    SetupPlan      = { programs: PlannedProgram[], toolTable: ToolTableEntry[],
                        toolLoads: number, toolChanges: number, warnings: string[] }
    planPrograms(setup): SetupPlan                            // pure: no geometry, no clock
    
    // programs.ts
    GeneratedProgram = { name, programNumber, gcode, program: Program, stats: GcodeStats,
                         warnings: string[], operationIds: string[], toolNumbers: number[] }
    GeneratedSetup   = { programs: GeneratedProgram[], toolTable: ToolTableEntry[],
                         totals: { programs, toolLoads, toolChanges, cutLength, rapidLength, estimatedSeconds },
                         warnings: string[] }
    generateSetup(setup, geometryOf: (op) => GeometryInput): GeneratedSetup
    ```
    
    Semantics:
    
    - **One emit per program.** Each operation is generated through the existing generator (`cam.generator.generateToolpath`), its IR moves become one `Toolpath` of a single `Program`, and that program is emitted **once** by the post — which already writes `G80`/`M5`/`M9`/`T<n> M6`/`G43 H<n>`/`M3 S…`/coolant per tool change (`post/emitter.ts`). No string concatenation anywhere.
    - **`toolId` is the Tool identity** (library id when there is one): `validate(program, machine)` already reports `MissingTool` against `machine.tools` by id. The pocket number is emission metadata, passed as the new `PostOptions.toolNumbers` override so a carousel's T numbers are honoured instead of first-use numbering.
    - **Ordering.** The setup order is the machining order. Within a `perTool` group the operations stay in setup order; `planPrograms` warns when a tool appears in non-adjacent operations (an avoidable extra tool change) and exposes `reorderByTool(operations)` for the UI to fix it.
    - **Retract before a tool change** (`retractBeforeToolChange`): raises Z to the highest Z the program reached before `M5`/`M9`/`T… M6`. Opt-in: the goldens of `04-core-cam.md` must not move.
    - **Program numbers.** `post.programNumberBase` (default 1000) + index; written into `metadata.programNumber` (read by the fanuc `O` number) and used in the program name.
    - **`fromPreviousStock`.** An operation that continues the tool before it gets that operation's real diameter as `restToolDiameter` instead of the hand-typed one, when it is a rest/rest-finishing `3d_surface` pass. It assumes the previous operation cut at the same Z levels with the same allowance (documented limit of `04-core-cam.md` §12); a height-field carryover between operations is out of scope.
    - **Validation.** `validateSetup(setup)` returns issues (duplicate pocket, an operation without elements, rpm above `tool.maxRpm`, a group with no enabled operation). It is reported, not thrown: the UI shows problems next to the offending row.
    
    ## 4. API (`packages/api`)
    
    New group `camSetup` (additive; the legacy routes of `05-api-sidecar.md` are untouched):
    
    | Route | Body | Result |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | `POST /api/cam/setups/plan` | `{ setup }` | `SetupPlan` (dry run, no geometry needed) |
    | `POST /api/cam/setups/generate` | `{ setup }` | `GeneratedSetup` |
    | `POST /api/cam/setups/validate` | `{ setup }` | `{ issues }` |
    
    `generate` resolves `elementIds` on the caller side (the request carries the resolved elements), because the core is I/O-free.
    
    ## 5. As built
    
    - `program/{op,setup,tooltable,plan,programs}.ts`, exported as `cam.program`.
    - `PostOptions.toolNumbers?: Record<string, number>` and `PostOptions.retractBeforeToolChange?: boolean` in `post/types.ts`; `emitProgram` applies both. Defaults keep the existing output byte-identical.
    - `analyseGcode` reports one `Toolpath` per tool change (the reference interpreter now returns tool changes with their line), so a multi-tool `.nc` parsed back keeps its tools for the viewport, the verifier and the AI tools.
    - Desktop (`setupStore`) and DB persistence follow in the next steps of this WP.
    
    ## 6. Change log
    
    - v1: contracts and semantics (this document).

## Activity

    $ cat /Users/nikoemme-os/.agents/skills/browser-control/SKILL.md; cat /Users/nikoemme-os/.agents/skills/synced/10f6d5c8-5fad-4a5f-aebd-84a61bc72627_53a32dae-8bfd-48bd-b0b9-cd5a059d7697/frontend-design/SKILL.md
    ---
    name: browser-control
    description: Autonomously control, inspect, test, and record real web pages through a background headless-browser daemon, producing screenshots, GIF, MP4, a manifest, and PR-ready Markdown. Use whenever the user asks to test, check, exercise, or verify a web UI, frontend flow, dashboard, or browser regression, including requests such as "testa la UI", "verifica questa pagina", "registra il flusso nel browser", or "includilo nella PR".
    ---
    
    # Browser Control
    
    Operate real, visible page state with `browser-control`. Never infer a UI outcome from server logs alone. Inside the nikcli monorepo, where the package binary is not installed globally, replace `browser-control` with `bun run --cwd packages/browser-control control --`.
    
    Sessions run headlessly in a per-workspace background daemon that survives across separate CLI calls (auto-spawned on first use, self-terminating after 10 idle minutes) — start a session in one command, drive and inspect it across several more, then stop it.
    
    ## Handle Requests Autonomously
    
    When the user asks to test a web UI, inspect the project to identify how to run it locally (dev server URL, port) and the acceptance criteria, then run the verification without asking for commands that are already discoverable. Translate the requested interactions into deterministic `wait`, `click`, `fill`, and `send` operations. Do not claim success unless a captured snapshot proves the expected result.
    
    Restarted nikcli processes discover this skill through `.agents/skills/browser-control`. If the skill is not registered in the current workspace, install it once from the worktree root:
    
    ```bash
    bun run --cwd packages/browser-control install-skill
    ```
    
    ## Run A Recorded Verification
    
    ```bash
    browser-control start ui-check --url http://localhost:3000 --viewport 1280x800 --record
    browser-control wait ui-check --text "Dashboard" --timeout 20000
    browser-control click ui-check "#open-settings"
    browser-control wait ui-check --selector "[data-testid=settings-panel]" --timeout 5000
    browser-control start-recording ui-check
    browser-control marker ui-check ready
    browser-control fill ui-check "#name" "hello"
    browser-control click ui-check "#save"
    browser-control wait ui-check --text "Saved" --timeout 10000
    browser-control marker ui-check verified
    browser-control snapshot ui-check --out /tmp/ui-check.png --format png
    browser-control stop-recording ui-check
    browser-control stop ui-check
    ```
    
    Use `wait` after every input instead of fixed sleeps: `--text VALUE` for visible text, `--selector SEL [--state visible|attached|hidden|detached]` for DOM presence, or `--idle` for network idle. Use `snapshot --format text` to read the page's accessibility tree when a screenshot alone won't prove the state (e.g. verifying an aria-live announcement or a form value).
    
    Always `stop` named sessions when done, including after failures — `stop` keeps the session queryable (for the `--record` video, only readable after `stop`) until you `remove` it or the daemon's idle timeout reclaims it. Treat recordings and typed input as potentially sensitive (auth flows, pasted credentials).
    
    ## Produce PR Evidence
    
    After stopping the session, fetch the video path if one was recorded and bundle it with the final screenshot:
    
    ```bash
    browser-control bundle \
      --screenshot /tmp/ui-check.png \
      --video "$(browser-control videoPath ui-check 2>/dev/null)" \
      --out artifacts/browser/ui-check \
      --link-base artifacts/browser/ui-check \
      --result passed \
      --title "UI verification" \
      --summary "The tested interaction completed successfully."
    ```
    
    The bundle contains `screenshot.png`, `demo.mp4`, `preview.gif`, `manifest.json`, and `pr.md`.
    
    Use `pr.md` as the PR section; it embeds the GIF preview and links the full MP4. The bundle command also prints an absolute `file://` preview Markdown line — include that exact line in the final assistant response so nikcli can render the local preview inline, but never paste that `file://` URL into a pull request.
    
    When the user explicitly requests PR evidence or the task is already operating on a pull request:
    
    1. Write the bundle under a stable repository path such as `artifacts/browser/<check-name>`.
    2. Inspect the screenshot, preview and manifest for secrets or sensitive account data before publishing.
    3. Commit and push the safe generated evidence.
    4. Use a public `--link-base` for the pushed artifact directory when available, then append the contents of `pr.md` to the existing PR description without discarding its current content.
    
    If no pull request exists, leave `pr.md` ready and report its path instead of creating unrelated remote state.
    
    If the test fails, still capture the final screenshot and video with `--result failed`, describe the observed failure accurately, and never report it as passed.
    
    ## Cleanup
    
    `browser-control close-all` stops every session in the current workspace and shuts down the daemon immediately — use it once evidence has been captured and there's nothing left to inspect. Otherwise the daemon exits on its own after 10 minutes with no running sessions.

    name: frontend-design
    description: Create distinctive, production-grade frontend interfaces with high design quality. Use this skill when the user asks to build web components, pages, or applications. Generates creative, polished code that avoids generic AI aesthetics.
    license: Complete terms in LICENSE.txt
    ---
    
    This skill guides creation of distinctive, production-grade frontend interfaces that avoid generic "AI slop" aesthetics. Implement real working code with exceptional attention to aesthetic details and creative choices.
    
    The user provides frontend requirements: a component, page, application, or interface to build. They may include context about the purpose, audience, or technical constraints.
    
    ## Design Thinking
    
    Before coding, understand the context and commit to a BOLD aesthetic direction:
    - **Purpose**: What problem does this interface solve? Who uses it?
    - **Tone**: Pick an extreme: brutally minimal, maximalist chaos, retro-futuristic, organic/natural, luxury/refined, playful/toy-like, editorial/magazine, brutalist/raw, art deco/geometric, soft/pastel, industrial/utilitarian, etc. There are so many flavors to choose from. Use these for inspiration but design one that is true to the aesthetic direction.
    - **Constraints**: Technical requirements (framework, performance, accessibility).
    - **Differentiation**: What makes this UNFORGETTABLE? What's the one thing someone will remember?
    
    **CRITICAL**: Choose a clear conceptual direction and execute it with precision. Bold maximalism and refined minimalism both work - the key is intentionality, not intensity.
    
    Then implement working code (HTML/CSS/JS, React, Vue, etc.) that is:
    - Production-grade and functional
    - Visually striking and memorable
    - Cohesive with a clear aesthetic point-of-view
    - Meticulously refined in every detail
    
    ## Frontend Aesthetics Guidelines
    
    Focus on:
    - **Typography**: Choose fonts that are beautiful, unique, and interesting. Avoid generic fonts like Arial and Inter; opt instead for distinctive choices that elevate the frontend's aesthetics; unexpected, characterful font choices. Pair a distinctive display font with a refined body font.
    - **Color & Theme**: Commit to a cohesive aesthetic. Use CSS variables for consistency. Dominant colors with sharp accents outperform timid, evenly-distributed palettes.
    - **Motion**: Use animations for effects and micro-interactions. Prioritize CSS-only solutions for HTML. Use Motion library for React when available. Focus on high-impact moments: one well-orchestrated page load with staggered reveals (animation-delay) creates more delight than scattered micro-interactions. Use scroll-triggering and hover states that surprise.
    - **Spatial Composition**: Unexpected layouts. Asymmetry. Overlap. Diagonal flow. Grid-breaking elements. Generous negative space OR controlled density.
    - **Backgrounds & Visual Details**: Create atmosphere and depth rather than defaulting to solid colors. Add contextual effects and textures that match the overall aesthetic. Apply creative forms like gradient meshes, noise textures, geometric patterns, layered transparencies, dramatic shadows, decorative borders, custom cursors, and grain overlays.
    
    NEVER use generic AI-generated aesthetics like overused font families (Inter, Roboto, Arial, system fonts), cliched color schemes (particularly purple gradients on white backgrounds), predictable layouts and component patterns, and cookie-cutter design that lacks context-specific character.
    
    Interpret creatively and make unexpected choices that feel genuinely designed for the context. No design should be the same. Vary between light and dark themes, different fonts, different aesthetics. NEVER converge on common choices (Space Grotesk, for example) across generations.
    
    **IMPORTANT**: Match implementation complexity to the aesthetic vision. Maximalist designs need elaborate code with extensive animations and effects. Minimalist or refined designs need restraint, precision, and careful attention to spacing, typography, and subtle details. Elegance comes from executing the vision well.
    
    Remember: Codex is capable of extraordinary creative work. Don't hold back, show what can truly be created when thinking outside the box and committing fully to a distinctive vision.

## Assistant

Il problema è nel formato salvato: conserva il G-code complessivo, ma perde l’elenco delle operazioni generate separatamente. Estendo il formato per conservarle e ripristinarle. Per i dettagli userò il visualizzatore 3D già presente, mantenendo lo stile dell’app; applico la skill `frontend-design` per questa vista.

    /**
     * The multi-toolpath store (legacy `multiToolpathStore.ts`, zustand + `persist`).
     *
     * State and actions are the reducers of `cam/lib/multiToolpath.ts`; `toolpaths` and `globalSettings` are persisted
     * to `localStorage` under the legacy key, the selection and the active toolpath reset with the session.
     */
    import { create } from "../../../shared/state/zustand.ts"
    import type { ToolpathSettings } from "../lib/elementJob.ts"
    import * as model from "../lib/multiToolpath.ts"
    import type { ElementToolpath, MultiToolpathState, ToolpathPoint } from "../lib/multiToolpath.ts"
    
    export type { ElementToolpath, ToolpathPoint } from "../lib/multiToolpath.ts"
    export { TOOLPATH_COLORS } from "../lib/multiToolpath.ts"
    
    export const STORAGE_KEY = "multi-toolpath-storage"
    
    interface MultiToolpathStore extends MultiToolpathState {
      addToolpath(elementId: string, elementType: string, elementName: string, settings: ToolpathSettings): string
      updateToolpath(id: string, updates: Partial<ElementToolpath>): void
      removeToolpath(id: string): void
      removeToolpathByElementId(elementId: string): void
      selectToolpath(id: string): void
      deselectToolpath(id: string): void
      selectAllToolpaths(): void
      deselectAllToolpaths(): void
      toggleToolpathVisibility(id: string): void
      showAllToolpaths(): void
      hideAllToolpaths(): void
      updateToolpathGCode(id: string, gcode: string, points: ReadonlyArray<ToolpathPoint>, measured?: { readonly distance: number; readonly time: number }): void
      reorderToolpaths(newOrder: ReadonlyArray<string>): void
      moveToolpathUp(id: string): void
      moveToolpathDown(id: string): void
      getToolpathByElementId(elementId: string): ElementToolpath | undefined
      getToolpathById(id: string): ElementToolpath | undefined
      getVisibleToolpaths(): ElementToolpath[]
      getSelectedToolpaths(): ElementToolpath[]
      generateCombinedGCode(): string
      clearAllToolpaths(): void
      updateGlobalSettings(settings: Partial<ToolpathSettings>): void
      applyGlobalSettingsToSelected(): void
    }
    
    const slice = (store: MultiToolpathState): MultiToolpathState => ({
      toolpaths: store.toolpaths,
      selectedToolpathIds: store.selectedToolpathIds,
      activeToolpathId: store.activeToolpathId,
      globalSettings: store.globalSettings
    })
    
    /** Persisted part of the state, tolerant of missing / corrupt storage. */
    const restore = (): Partial<MultiToolpathState> => {
      try {
        const raw = localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KEY)
        if (raw === null) return {}
        const parsed = JSON.parse(raw) as { state?: { toolpaths?: unknown; globalSettings?: unknown } }
        const toolpaths = Array.isArray(parsed.state?.toolpaths) ? (parsed.state!.toolpaths as ElementToolpath[]).map(toolpath => ({ ...toolpath, selected: false })) : []
        const globalSettings = typeof parsed.state?.globalSettings === "object" && parsed.state.globalSettings !== null ? (parsed.state.globalSettings as Partial<ToolpathSettings>) : model.INITIAL_GLOBAL_SETTINGS
        return { toolpaths, globalSettings }
      } catch {
        return {}
      }
    }
    
    export const useMultiToolpathStore = create<MultiToolpathStore>((set, get) => {
      const apply = (reduce: (state: MultiToolpathState) => MultiToolpathState): void => { set(reduce(slice(get()))) }
      return {
        ...model.initialState(),
        ...restore(),
    
        addToolpath: (elementId, elementType, elementName, settings) => {
          const id = model.newToolpathId()
          apply(state => model.addToolpath(state, { id, elementId, elementType, elementName, settings }))
          return id
        },
        updateToolpath: (id, updates) => apply(state => model.updateToolpath(state, id, updates)),
        removeToolpath: id => apply(state => model.removeToolpath(state, id)),
        removeToolpathByElementId: elementId => apply(state => model.removeToolpathsByElementId(state, elementId)),
    
        selectToolpath: id => apply(state => model.selectToolpath(state, id)),
        deselectToolpath: id => apply(state => model.deselectToolpath(state, id)),
        selectAllToolpaths: () => apply(model.selectAllToolpaths),
        deselectAllToolpaths: () => apply(model.deselectAllToolpaths),
    
        toggleToolpathVisibility: id => apply(state => model.toggleToolpathVisibility(state, id)),
        showAllToolpaths: () => apply(state => model.setAllVisible(state, true)),
        hideAllToolpaths: () => apply(state => model.setAllVisible(state, false)),
    
        updateToolpathGCode: (id, gcode, points, measured) => apply(state => model.updateToolpathGCode(state, id, gcode, points, measured)),
    
        reorderToolpaths: newOrder => apply(state => model.reorderToolpaths(state, newOrder)),
        moveToolpathUp: id => apply(state => model.moveToolpathUp(state, id)),
        moveToolpathDown: id => apply(state => model.moveToolpathDown(state, id)),
    
        getToolpathByElementId: elementId => model.toolpathByElementId(get(), elementId),
        getToolpathById: id => get().toolpaths.find(toolpath => toolpath.id === id),
        getVisibleToolpaths: () => get().toolpaths.filter(toolpath => toolpath.visible),
        getSelectedToolpaths: () => get().toolpaths.filter(toolpath => toolpath.selected),
        generateCombinedGCode: () => model.combineGCode(get().toolpaths),
    
        clearAllToolpaths: () => apply(state => ({ ...state, toolpaths: [], selectedToolpathIds: [], activeToolpathId: null })),
    
        updateGlobalSettings: settings => apply(state => ({ ...state, globalSettings: { ...state.globalSettings, ...settings } })),
        applyGlobalSettingsToSelected: () => apply(model.applyGlobalSettingsToSelected)
      }
    })
    
    // Persist `toolpaths` and `globalSettings` (the legacy `partialize`), in zustand's `persist` envelope.
    useMultiToolpathStore.subscribe(state => {
      try {
        localStorage.setItem(STORAGE_KEY, JSON.stringify({ state: { toolpaths: state.toolpaths, globalSettings: state.globalSettings }, version: 0 }))
      } catch { /* storage blocked or full: the toolpaths live for this session */ }
    })

    export type ViewPreset = "perspective" | "top" | "front" | "right" | "isometric" | "bottom" | "back" | "left" | "four-view"
    export type RenderMode = "shaded" | "shaded-edges" | "wireframe" | "xray"
    export type TransformMode = "none" | "translate" | "rotate" | "scale"
    export interface ViewportTransform {

      readonly axesVisible?: boolean

      readonly stock?: { readonly origin: { readonly x: number; readonly y: number; readonly z: number }; readonly size: { readonly x: number; readonly y: number; readonly z: number } }

      readonly onRenderModeChange?: (mode: RenderMode) => void
      readonly onSolidSelect?: (index: number | undefined) => void

      readonly lighting?: ViewportLighting

      readonly section?: ViewportSection

        readonly showPointLabels: boolean

        readonly stockVisible: boolean
        readonly stockOpacity: number
        readonly arcTolerance: number
      }
    }
    
    export const disposeGroup = (group: THREE.Object3D): void => {
      group.traverse(object => {
        if (object instanceof THREE.Mesh || object instanceof THREE.Line || object instanceof THREE.Sprite) {
          object.geometry.dispose()
          for (const material of Array.isArray(object.material) ? object.material : [object.material]) {
            if (material instanceof THREE.SpriteMaterial) material.map?.dispose()
            material.dispose()
          }
        }
      })
      group.clear()
    }
    
    export function View3D(props: View3DProps) {
      let host: HTMLDivElement | undefined
      // A webview (or a test DOM) without WebGL must not take the whole screen down with it.
      const [noWebGl, setNoWebGl] = createSignal(false)
      const [meshError, setMeshError] = createSignal<string>()
      let invalidate = () => {}
      const [preset, updatePreset] = createSignal<ViewPreset>(props.preset ?? "perspective")
      const [mode, updateMode] = createSignal<RenderMode>(props.renderMode ?? "shaded-edges")
      const [gridVisible, setGridVisible] = createSignal(props.gridVisible ?? true)
      const [axesVisible, setAxesVisible] = createSignal(props.axesVisible ?? true)
      const [transformMode, setTransformMode] = createSignal<TransformMode>("none")
      const [selected, setSelected] = createSignal<number>()
      let fit = () => {}, appearance = () => {}, transform = () => {}, applyLighting = () => {}, applyMaterial = () => {}, applySection = () => {}
      let rebuild = () => {}, setPreset = () => {}
      let playback: Playback = { segments: [], duration: 0, end: { x: 0, y: 0, z: 0 } }
      let seconds = 0
      createEffect(() => { props.drawing; props.solids; props.extrusionHeight; props.program; props.stock; props.toolpathDisplay; untrack(rebuild) })
      createEffect(() => { if (props.preset) updatePreset(props.preset) })
      createEffect(() => { preset(); untrack(setPreset) })
      createEffect(() => { if (props.renderMode) updateMode(props.renderMode) })
      createEffect(() => { if (props.gridVisible !== undefined) setGridVisible(props.gridVisible) })
      createEffect(() => { if (props.axesVisible !== undefined) setAxesVisible(props.axesVisible) })
      createEffect(() => { mode(); gridVisible(); axesVisible(); props.origin; untrack(appearance) })
      createEffect(() => { transformMode(); selected(); untrack(transform) })
      createEffect(() => { seconds = props.playbackTime ?? 0; props.playing; invalidate() })
      createEffect(() => { props.lighting; untrack(applyLighting) })
      createEffect(() => { props.material; untrack(applyMaterial) })
      createEffect(() => { props.section; untrack(applySection) })
      onMount(() => {
        if (!host) return
    /**
     * Mounts the whole UI (`AppRoutes`) over the *real* sidecar handlers: the generated typed
     * client talks to the in-process HTTP handler backed by an in-memory SQLite database, and
     * the DOM is happy-dom. No browser, no network, no fake API.
     */
    import { clientLayer, readClient, runtimeForLayer } from "../../src/app/client.ts"
    import { makeApp } from "../../../sidecar/test/helpers.ts"
    import { installDom } from "./dom.ts"
    import { resetStores } from "./reset-stores.ts"
    
    export async function bootLiveApp() {
      const sidecar = await makeApp()
      installDom("http://localhost/")
      // The Zustand stores are module-level singletons that survive a new DOM window, so wipe
      // them here: a previous test file in this process may have left elements, projects or a
      // remembered document in `useElementsStore` / `useDocumentStore`, and the next file's first
      // `prepareWorkspace` would otherwise see that stale state.
      resetStores()
      const { render } = await import("solid-js/web")
      const { createEffect, createRoot } = await import("solid-js")
      const { AppRoutes } = await import("../../src/app/App.tsx")
      const { createAppState } = await import("../../src/shared/state/context.ts")
      const { watchSystemTheme } = await import("../../src/shared/state/settings.ts")
      const runtime = runtimeForLayer(clientLayer({ baseUrl: "http://sidecar.test", token: "test-token" }, sidecar.transport))
      const client = await readClient(runtime)
      const state = createAppState({ client, runtime, sidecar: "ready" })
      // The theme is applied by `App`, which this harness does not mount: wire the same effect here.
      const theme = watchSystemTheme()
      createRoot(() => createEffect(() => theme.apply(state.settings.settings().theme)))
    
      const wait = async <T>(read: () => T | null | undefined | false, timeoutMs = 4000): Promise<T> => {
        const until = Date.now() + timeoutMs
        for (;;) {
          const value = read()
          if (value) return value
          if (Date.now() > until) throw new Error(`timed out; page text: ${document.body.textContent?.slice(0, 300)}`)
          await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 10))
        }
      }
      /** Navigates to `path` and mounts a fresh copy of the app there; returns its container. */
      const visit = (path: string) => {
        window.history.pushState({}, "", path)
        const root = document.createElement("div")
        document.body.replaceChildren(root)
        const dispose = render(() => AppRoutes({ state }), root)
        return { root, dispose }
      }
      const dispose = async () => { theme.dispose(); state.dispose(); await sidecar.dispose() }
      return { sidecar, client, state, visit, wait, dispose }
    }

    # WP6 + WP7 — Desktop UI (`apps/desktop`)
    
    ## Component creation and library round-trip (2026-10-13)
    
    Status: in progress. WP6 owns this editor increment in `features/cad/{components,lib,store,screens}`, `features/library/hooks`, locale entries and desktop tests. Create Component from the toolbar or canvas opens one in-editor dialog for the complete selection: name, description, tags, target layer, original retention and optional library saving (enabled by default), with local or project destination and project visibility. All descendant components and groups remain nested, with geometry, poses and metadata preserved. Preview the included hierarchy and counts. Persistence failures keep the dialog and original scene intact; creation is one undo step. Library placement and preview must use the complete stored geometry with fresh instance IDs, including legacy single-element records and project documents. Existing typed API contracts are reused.
    
    
    ## Assistant controls (2026-10-10)
    
    See `24-assistant-control-flow.md`: WP6 adds role/domain/thinking selectors, manual context compaction, interactive native question replies and background task cancellation in the existing assistant. Controls remain bound to the active session/project and generated API; context follows host limits, with unknown values explicit. Status: in progress. Change log: 2026-10-10 — registered assistant control UI ownership before implementation.
    
    ## Bootstrap and graphics performance alignment (2026-10-09)
    
    WP6/WP7 follow-up from `23-ai-runtime-architecture.md`: each bootstrap attempt owns late-acquired resources and cancellable network work; credentials belong to a client instance. Meshing jobs are bounded, errors/timeouts close pending work, superseded scene work is terminated, and static views avoid redundant GPU rendering. Preserve existing controls, editor behavior and display appearance. CADCanvas owns a demand-driven frame scheduler: idle scenes schedule no frames, hidden documents suspend rendering, and orbit damping wakes only until settled. Scene teardown releases owned geometries, materials and textures once and cancels pointer/fullscreen callbacks.
    
    
    Status: core flows implemented and tested (83 desktop tests). Boot/runtime with the generated typed client and the browser fake, the app shell, and the Projects, CAD, CAM, Library and Settings screens are working end to end against the contract; the 3D viewport plays back toolpaths, and the 2D editor drives the tool state machines. Remaining work is the long tail of legacy pages, which are still generated stubs rather than implemented screens.
    
    Visual status (2026-10-08): compact CAD/CAM workstation chrome implemented in WP6-owned components; geometry, state and viewport rendering remain unchanged.
    
    Both workspaces also share the generator inspector's blue glass tab treatment and the CAD quick-navigation menu, now available at the bottom of the CAM sidebar. Navigation and panel content scroll independently; collapse controls stay available.
    
    - **WP6** owns `apps/desktop/src/app/`, `features/*/screens/`, `shared/components/`, `shared/state/`, `src/i18n/` — app shell, navigation, project/library screens, forms, API wiring.
    - **WP7** owns `apps/desktop/src/viewport/` — 2D drawing canvas, 3D viewport, toolpath preview.
    - Shared by both (edited only by whoever creates it first, then frozen): `apps/desktop/package.json`, `vite.config.ts`, `index.html`, `src/main.tsx`.
    
    Stack: **SolidJS** (`solid-js`, `@solidjs/router`, `vite-plugin-solid`), TypeScript strict, Tailwind CSS, Solid signals/stores for UI state, plain Three.js (no React wrappers) for 3D, Canvas2D or Pixi for the 2D editor (WP7 decides, documents it here). **Parity requirement:** the UI is visually and functionally identical to the legacy React UI (`.reference/src/components`, `.reference/src/pages`, `.reference/src/styles`, tailwind config, fonts and icons from `public/`) — same layout, screens, routes, copy, shortcuts and styling — re-implemented component-by-component in Solid (no React, no Next.js). Tests use `@solidjs/testing-library`. Data access only through `ApiClient` from `@cadcamfun/api` (typed, Effect-based); the UI runs effects with a single app `ManagedRuntime`.
    
    Legacy reference (behaviour/layout inspiration only): `.reference/src/components/{cad,cam,layout,library,materials,production-costs,simulation,settings,ai}/`, `.reference/public/cursors/`, `.reference/src/store/`, `.reference/src/hooks/canvas/`.
    
    ## 1. App shell (WP6)
    
    - Boot sequence: Tauri command `get_sidecar_endpoint` → `{ baseUrl, token }` → build `ApiClient.layer` → `GET /health` → render. Show a splash with error and "Retry"/"View logs" only after the sidecar has stayed unreachable for 150 s: until then the splash keeps re-resolving the endpoint and retrying (the shell waits up to 60 s for each start, and the host's SDK is loaded lazily so the API answers first), showing the seconds waited and a readable reason.
    - Layout: title bar (native menus from the shell, in-window fallback on Windows/Linux), left activity bar (Projects, CAD, CAM, Library, Costs, AI, Plugins, Settings), main area, bottom status bar (units, snap state, cursor coords, sidecar status).
    - Screens: **Projects** (list/create/open/rename/delete, recent), **CAD editor** (WP7 canvas + tool palette, layers panel, properties panel), **CAM workspace** (operation list, parameters form generated from WP2 Schemas, toolpath preview, G-code view with export), **Library** (materials, tools, machine configs, components), **Costs** (estimate form + breakdown), **AI panel** (docked chat, streaming), **Plugins**, **Settings** (units, theme, locale, post-processor defaults).
    - Forms are generated from the Effect Schemas where possible (one small `SchemaForm` component) so parameters never drift from the core definitions.
    - State: server data lives in the API (no duplicated caches beyond a lightweight query cache keyed by endpoint + params); undo/redo history for the editor is client state (command pattern over `Drawing` patches, max 200 steps).
    - Theming: light/dark/system via CSS variables; every colour as a token; keyboard-first (command palette `Cmd/Ctrl+K`, shortcuts table in settings).
    - i18n: `src/i18n/en.json` only for v6.0, keyed strings, no inline literals in components (lint rule).
    - Accessibility: all interactive controls keyboard reachable and labelled; focus ring visible.
    
    ## 2. Viewport (WP7)
    
    2D editor (`viewport/editor2d/`):
    - Pan/zoom (wheel, pinch, space-drag), grid (dots/lines, major spacing) and rulers, units-aware.
    - Tools: select (click, box, add/remove), line, polyline, rectangle, circle, arc (3-point and centre-start-end), ellipse, text, dimension, move/rotate/scale/mirror, offset, trim/extend, fillet/chamfer — each tool is a small state machine `{ onPointerDown, onPointerMove, onPointerUp, onKey, preview() }` that emits **commands** (pure patches) executed against the document; tools never mutate state directly.
    - Snapping uses `findSnap` from `@cadcamfun/core`; render snap markers; 60 fps with 10 000 entities (spatial index: uniform grid or R-tree in `viewport/spatial/`).
    - Cursors from `public/cursors/*.svg` (kept in the repo).
    
    3D viewport (`viewport/view3d/`):
    - Three.js scene with orbit/pan/zoom, view presets (perspective, top, front, right, isometric, four-view), axes gizmo, grid plane, stock box, workpiece origin marker.
    - Renders `meshOf(solid)` output from WP1 and tessellated drawing entities extruded on demand.
    - Toolpath preview: draws the WP2 IR as line segments (rapids dashed/colour-coded, cuts solid, arcs tessellated), with a scrub slider and play/pause animating a tool marker along the path; no material-removal simulation in v6.0.
    - All geometry buffers built off the main thread when > 50 000 vertices (Worker with transferable `Float32Array`).
    
    Performance budget: first interactive paint < 1.5 s after sidecar ready on a 2020 laptop; no frame over 16 ms during pan/zoom with the reference drawing in `apps/desktop/test/fixtures/large-drawing.json` (WP7 creates it).
    
    ## 3. Testing
    
    - Unit tests (`bun test`) for tool state machines, command patches, undo/redo, SchemaForm, spatial index, cursor→snap pipeline (no DOM).
    - Component tests with `@solidjs/testing-library` + `happy-dom` for screens against an in-memory fake `ApiClient`.
    - One end-to-end smoke test (Playwright against the Vite dev server with the fake API) covering: create project → draw rectangle → create profile operation → see G-code.
    - WP6/WP7 develop against a **fake `ApiClient`** (in-memory `Layer`) until WP4 lands; the fake lives in `apps/desktop/src/testing/fake-api.ts`.
    
    ## 4. Acceptance criteria
    
    - `bun run --filter @cadcamfun/desktop dev` serves the UI in a browser with the fake API; `build` produces a static bundle for Tauri.
    - All 2D tools above work with undo/redo, and the resulting `Drawing` round-trips through the WP1 Schema.
    - Keyboard-only workflow for create project → draw → generate → export works.
    
    ## 5. Change log
    
    - 2026-10-11: the WP6 assistant surface now owns a stable, context-aware header and a compact Codex-style composer. Project/drawing and companion-device disclosures use real typed API data; slash commands are keyboard-operable; proactive suggestions use dismissible toast cards; agent, domain, permission and thinking controls remain visible as a spaced icon rail whose custom menus open upward; and the model menu is right-anchored so it grows left inside narrow docks. Composer popovers sit above transcript toasts without clipping, while light/dark text fields and menus share the assistant surface tokens. The empty state and responsive layout were refined for both the full AI page and the docked CAD/CAM panel.
    
    - 2026-10-11: the CAM Toolpath visualizer restores the legacy top-right visualization settings control. The Solid/Three.js implementation exposes live statistics, filterable move points and optional labels, point sizing, arc-center and radius helpers, arc tessellation tolerance, plus workpiece visibility and opacity. Inspection geometry is derived from the typed CAM IR in `src/viewport/view3d/toolpath-overlays.ts`; it is rendered by the shared demand-driven `View3D` scene and covered by focused geometry tests.
    
    - 2026-10-11: the legacy Custom Elements subsystem is ported to the CAD editor (WP6-owned `features/cad/`, plus locale entries in `src/i18n/en.cad.json`). `features/cad/types/customElements.ts`, `features/cad/lib/customElements/{evaluate,templates}.ts` and `features/cad/store/customElementsStore.ts` carry the definitions, the geometry-script runner and the persisted store (same storage key as the legacy app); `features/cad/components/custom-elements/` holds the editor (code with the legacy snippet buttons, parameters, settings), the saved-elements browser, the parameter controls and the Three.js preview. The toolbar gains a Custom Elements button, and "Add to scene" places the element as `type: "custom"`, which `createThreeObject` draws by running its script with the saved parameters. Geometry bodies are the legacy ones verbatim; the React UI is re-expressed in Solid with the shared `Modal` kit.

The AI tab opens the existing assistant dock.

The 54px strip exposes existing modeling operations, booleans and section controls, with parameters in the inspector; no geometry or renderer changes are required. WP6 also owns the container-responsive layout of `features/cam/components/MachineCycles.tsx`: stacked configuration at sidebar widths, readable cycle rows and two columns only in wide containers.

Route audit: `/components` and `/components/:id` use `legacy-components.tsx`; `/materials`, `/tools`, `/machine` use `library.tsx` and their detail routes use `library-detail-screen.tsx` (separately owned); `/toolpaths` and `/toolpaths/:id` both still use `screens/cam.tsx`, left unchanged to preserve the CAM workspace.
    - 2026-10-08: modest CAD/CAM visual polish, preserving blue accents and light/dark identity. Shared `components/workstation.css` reduces toolbar padding, panel gaps, shadows and radii, aligns 12px control typography, and gives properties/generator sections clearer hierarchy. Sidebars use responsive CSS widths without delayed slide-away transforms; narrow windows retain a 320px minimum stage in a horizontally scrollable workspace with all collapse controls preserved. CAD browser type metadata moves below the name, CAM inline assignment actions wrap, and shared row actions remain visible on keyboard focus and touch. No geometry, state, API, or viewport rendering changes; existing dirty `AdvancedSection.tsx` and `project-detail.tsx` edits are untouched.
    - v1: initial spec.
    - Editor core implemented and tested without a canvas or a DOM (`bun test`, 54 tests):
      - `shared/state/commands.ts` + `shared/state/history.ts`: the command pattern and a
        snapshot-based undo/redo capped at the spec's 200 steps. `undo` walks recorded
        documents backwards instead of deriving inverses, so an entity edited twice can
        never be restored to the wrong state; dropping the oldest steps keeps the base
        document and reports how many were dropped.
      - `src/viewport/spatial/grid.ts`: exact uniform-grid index. Items too large to
        enumerate per cell (a stock box) are tracked aside and still returned by every
        query; 10 000 entities build and query without a linear scan.
      - `src/viewport/editor2d/tools/`: tools as `{ onPointerDown, onPointerMove,
        onPointerUp, onKey, preview, command, status }` state machines that return
        `Command`s and never touch the document. Shift constrains lines and rectangles,
        gesture ids never collide, and a polyline commits only once it has two points.
      - `shared/components/schema-form.ts`: derives the CAM parameter form from the WP2
        `Schema.Class` AST — kinds, enum literals, bounds (including the *exclusive*
        `isGreaterThan` bounds used by `totalDepth`/`stepdown`) and which fields have
        constructor defaults. Decoding goes through `decodeUnknownExit`, so a bad value
        can never throw inside the editor.
    - Conventions learned the hard way: `mirror(entity, origin, angle)` reflects about
      the line **through** `origin` at `angle` radians, so a vertical axis is `π/2`;
      `Schema.optional(X)` is a `Union` of `X` and `Undefined`, which the form
      description has to unwrap.
    - Integration screens for the groups the sidecar now serves, all through the typed `ApiClient` (`features/*/screens/`): `plugins.tsx` (`/plugins` list with enable/disable and zip install via multipart `FormData`, `/plugins/:id` with manifest, JSON configuration and uninstall), `costs.tsx` (`/costs`: pick a toolpath, calculate, save, saved estimates), `organizations.tsx` (`/organizations`, `/create`, `/:id`), `waitlist.tsx`, `auth.tsx` (forgot password, magic link, reset password, verify e-mail; they show the development link the sidecar echoes when `CADCAMFUN_DEBUG_LINKS` is on) and `analytics.tsx` (`/analytics`, sidecar metrics). `/costs` was missing from the route table and is now registered. `apps/sidecar/test/typed-client.test.ts` runs the generated client against the real handlers (including the multipart install), so a contract drift fails a test instead of a screen. Not covered yet: the in-memory fake (`testing/fake-api.ts`) still answers 501 for these groups, so they only work against a live sidecar; no DOM component tests exist for them.
    - Model browser and status strips (CAD and CAM share them):
      - `cad/lib/browser.ts` holds the rules (Bodies / Sketches / Components by element type, grouping by type / layer / flat, isolate, show all, rename, bulk toggle); `cad/components/browser/` holds the shared state (`createBrowser()`) and the pieces (`BrowserToolbar`, `BrowserSections`, `RowActions`, `RenameName`, `OriginBlock`). The CAD `CADElementExplorer` and the CAM `CamElementExplorer` both render them, so every function (rename in place, lock, hide, duplicate, delete, isolate, show all, group / ungroup, move to layer, layer create / activate / hide / lock / delete, axes / grid / dimensions toggles) exists in both sidebars. CAM keeps its own tool, material and generate controls.
      - `components/workspace-strip.tsx` is the one-row (`h-6`, never wrapping) information strip. CAD (`StatusBar`): mode, transform tool, material, stock, grid / axes / dimensions toggles, layer, counts, selection (type, id, size), cursor, shortcuts. CAM (`CamStatusBar`): view, lines, moves, cut length, time, min Z, tool, rpm, feed, material, stock, post, work offset, selection, last verification verdict, simulation clock. The shell footer keeps units, snap and the sidecar state.
      - CAM sidebar "Verify" tab (`VerifyPanel`): holder fields (blank = not modelled), the verdict, issues worst first, volumes, the remaining-stock height map with gouges in red, a stale marker when the G-code changed after the report.
      - Assistant integration: the CAD and CAM pages share the open document with the assistant (`documentStore`), the assistant's context is built from the live stores, its CAD and CAM tool results are mirrored into the editor, the generator, the G-code editor and the Verify tab, and the browser toolbar and the Verify tab have "ask the assistant" buttons (spec 08, "Workspace integration"). The CAM page's editor follows `camStore.gcode`, so G-code set by the assistant appears without reloading.
      - Units and tolerance: the display unit (mm, µm, cm, m, inch, thou; everything stays millimetres inside) and the drawing tolerance are selected in the "Document & Origin" block of both sidebars and shown in the strip. `state/document-settings.ts` is the one place that changes them: settings, the 3D view (curves are meshed to the tolerance), the CAM generator and the verification default, and the stored drawing carry the same values.
      - Generator panel by machine: the origin section shows the mill's work offset and origin, the lathe's Z zero, or the printer's plate origin and size; mills can pick a touch probe or tool setter and the probing operation with its cycle settings.
      - Request bodies built from editor elements go through `state/json-safe.ts`, so an element holding `undefined`, NaN, typed arrays or cycles can no longer stop the generator ("Expected JSON value at ...").
    
    ## Folder roles (`src/app`, `src/pages`, `src/features`, `src/shared`)
    
    Owner-requested kernel integration (2026-10-14): enrich the current CAM generator with a native-kernel section, preserving its operation generator. Selection comes from the CAD stores; the authenticated sidecar returns the kernel program and evidence. Loading the result updates the existing editor and preview, resets previous verification and guards against stale geometry/settings/owner responses. Show stale evidence after NC edits. CADCanvas uses kernel geometry with one placement/gizmo transform and independently owned render resources.
    
    Owner-requested CAM Save (2026-10-14): the existing toolbar/ribbon Save and generator Save as Toolpath open one dialog. Keep G-code export; add a portable, validated JSON toolpath containing the exact NC, parsed program, generator settings, stock and native artifact when its NC matches. Save to an existing project or create a private project with a captured CAD drawing; do not overwrite an existing drawing implicitly. Reopening from the toolpath library fetches the stored original and restores the program/settings. Updating that saved program preserves its previous data/NC through the existing version API before updating. A separate saved-program reference must not confuse preview path selection with a persisted toolpath ID. Preserve unrelated saved metadata; changed NC drops obsolete kernel/verification evidence. Existing toolpath detail/library pages manage versions, rename and deletion. Desktop owner scope includes `features/cam`, the toolpath hand-off, `features/ai/lib/cam-sync.ts`, CAM i18n and their tests; no new persistence model or migration.
    
    Follow-up owner requirements: CAD and CAM export/save-project dialogs use the same shared layout, matching the CAD screenshot (file name/format, blue project card, Cancel/Export File footer). Project forms share name/description/organization/visibility, with additional destination/version controls for CAM and a rich contents summary for both editors. Owner scope additionally includes `shared/{ui,components}`, CAD export/project dialogs and their tests, i18n, and the shared UnifiedLibraryModal. The library overlay is portalled outside transformed workstation containers and must display its panel as well as its backdrop in both CAD and CAM; verify actual visible browser layout, not only DOM presence.
    
    CAM editing additionally owns bounded session history: Undo/Redo buttons and Cmd/Ctrl-Z, Shift-Z/Y restore NC, IR, operation results, native source/artifact, machining settings and stock as one snapshot. Playback stops and unrelated verification resets on restore. Parsing an edit updates that edit's program instead of adding a misleading separate operation. New edits after undo truncate redo; history is scoped to the active document and includes a selectable CAM History dialog. Saved DB versions remain managed through toolpath version APIs. Toolbar controls in both editors share their compact geometry (30 px control height, 16 px icons and 4 px gaps), including the new history controls.
    
    - `src/app/` is the shell: `App.tsx`, `Shell.tsx`, `routes.tsx` (the route table), the API client and global services. Nothing feature-specific.
    - `src/pages/` holds one-line route adapters, one file per legacy Next.js page path (66 routes; `test/app-mount.test.ts` checks the route table against these files). A page only re-exports a screen or a feature page; it contains no logic.
    - `src/features/<name>/` is one folder per product area, with the same inner layout everywhere: `screens/` (route-level views), `components/`, `hooks/`, `lib/` (pure logic), `store/`, `types/`, `api/` and `styles/` only when the feature needs them. Current areas: `ai`, `analytics`, `auth`, `billing`, `cad`, `cam`, `chat`, `companion`, `costs`, `dashboard`, `legal`, `library`, `marketplace`, `mcp`, `organizations`, `plugins`, `profile`, `projects`, `settings`, `toolpaths`, `admin`.
    - `src/shared/` holds code used by several features: `ui/` (primitives such as button, modal, card), `components/` (shell chrome such as title bar, status bar, workspace navigation, screen parts), `state/` (session, settings, query cache, editor state).
    - `src/styles/` holds the global stylesheets. Feature-specific CSS lives in `features/<name>/styles/`.
    - `src/viewport/` (WP7) is the 2D/3D engine; `src/i18n/` the translation tables; `src/testing/` the fake API and core-flow tests.
    - New logic goes in a feature folder or in `shared/` when it is used by two features, never in `pages/`.
    - Sessions and billing: `shared/state/session.ts` keeps the session token (a `Ref` read per request by the client middleware, mirrored to `localStorage`) and `startSession` / `endSession` switch accounts and drop the query cache. Sign-in, sign-up (signs in right after creating the account), magic-link verification and sign-out use them; the navbar shows the acting account with Sign in / Sign out. The pricing page renders the same plan catalogue as the subscription card (`features/billing/lib/plans.ts`), marks the current plan, starts the Pro trial through `POST /api/subscriptions` and says plainly when paid checkout is unavailable.
    
    Secondary resources follow nikcli ownership: queries accept cancellation through the app Effect runtime, superseded requests and unmounted views interrupt their fibers, and hidden CAD plugin panels do not fetch a registry. AI catalogue resources are loaded when their accessor is first used; connected models are preferred, and providers are sorted by configured status.
    
    - 2026-10-10: compact CAD commands also expose Edit (Duplicate / Copy / Paste), Parametric part, Sketch (Line / Circle / Rectangle) and Solids (Box / Cylinder / Sphere / Cone / Torus), reusing ElementForm and the element clipboard. Creation respects the active layer lock. Remaining Italian user-facing strings in CAD simulation, CAM generator examples/errors, and settings feedback are translated to English.
